This document expands every actual declaration: **165 requirements, 47 acceptance tests and 104 catalog entries = 316 primary rows**, plus 30 explicitly declared toggle keys and supplementary Governor clauses/case mappings. The frozen [task-5 interface contract](../../.arc-verify/governor/contract.md#amendment-13-traceability-format-and-counts), [task-8 integrated receipt](../../.arc-verify/governor/integration.json) and its `task13RepairAttempt2` correction are consumed without modification. The current format requires `Complete`, `Partial`, `Pending` or `Blocked`; each primary row carries Source, one primary Phase, Status, Evidence and Cycle. Requirements and acceptance cross-references use their respective inventories. `None` always explains the unmapped obligation. Catalog IDs retain their separate inventory and related mappings.

Attempt 15 repaired the ordinary cache-reuse evidence. The built `arcctl classify --json` sends an opt-in observation through a separate inherited Unix datagram socket for each fixture invocation. The observation contains only the executed result branch and its four classification fields. The target asserts a computed BUILD on the first invocation, a directly observed recorded-result BUILD on the second, a computed TEST after the manifest edit, and a directly observed recorded-result TEST on the next. Each observation is compared with that same invocation's ordinary output; ordinary stderr is empty, classification runs no fixture script, and diagnostic classification and `exec` agree with the corresponding results. Missing observations fail. As a mutation check, temporarily bypassing the recorded-result branch for this fixture failed the second-invocation assertion with `computed` versus `recorded`; the bypass was removed before the passing run.

On the repaired source, `CARGO_NET_OFFLINE=true cargo test -p arc-daemon --test detector_workspace -- --nocapture` passed 2 tests in **6.78 s** and reported the non-UTF-8 classify/exec comparison VERIFIED. The unchanged `cargo test -p arc-detector --test script_resolution` passed 12 tests; `cargo test -p arc-daemon --test phase0_targets nfr_005_cached_ordinary_classification_p95_within_50_ms` passed. `cargo test -p arc-daemon --test process_attribution -- --nocapture` passed 3 tests in **35.57 s**, logging same-PID replacement across consecutive scans and no observed real zero-total interval. `cargo test -p arc-daemon --test meter_quality -- --nocapture` passed 2 tests in **29.96 s**. The existing `job_process_meter::mtr_002_unwaited_grandchild_cpu_memory_and_io_are_attributed_to_the_job` passed in 6.65 s, and `cargo test -p arc-storage --lib attribution::tests::a_zero_total_and_skipped_processes_are_flagged` passed the deterministic zero-denominator case. All commands used `CARGO_NET_OFFLINE=true`. Scoped Clippy passed with warnings denied for `arc-cli --bin arcctl` and the daemon `detector_workspace` target; targeted rustfmt and `git diff --check` passed. A direct count of the 316 unique primary inventory rows gives the phase counts above. The selected row statuses reflect the production-path evidence; runner-owned gates on this changed snapshot remain unverified.

The pre-repair runner receipt for `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` passed in **1,012.468 s** on 2026-11-23, with matching input hash `d7265df0ca476b031c639b7ecb8058a239e7e31f2e0d99ed34a0d6ed2427a6fe`. The associated traceability receipt passed, and the coverage-gated harness passed using coverage receipt `2026-11-23T15-31-38-573Z-f1accf62-5cf7-4d13-8638-859765001064`; all 11 crates exceeded 80%. The workspace log recorded NFR-001 and the NFR-002 host test passing. These receipts precede the cache-observation repair and do not establish the current-tree gates. The runner must rerun the unchanged four-command contract, NFR-001, NFR-002, the workspace duration gate, traceability, per-crate coverage, and the coverage-gated architecture harness.

Before this recovery the focused production-path checks passed: `process_attribution` 3 tests in 35.67 s, `meter_quality` 2 tests in 29.93 s, and `detector_workspace` 2 tests in 6.46 s. The unchanged `script_resolution` passed 12 tests, NFR-005 passed, and `job_process_meter::mtr_002_unwaited_grandchild_cpu_memory_and_io_are_attributed_to_the_job` passed in 6.65 s. The attribution run logged a same-PID replacement across consecutive native scans; no real zero-total interval was observed. The arc-storage test `attribution::tests::a_zero_total_and_skipped_processes_are_flagged` passed separately for a zero denominator. The detector run reported the non-UTF-8 classify/exec comparison as VERIFIED. The Meter target also exercises the old-peer v1 request, response and CLI byte comparisons, successful job-specific privacy diagnostics, and classified queued/granted BUILD footprint. Earlier focused checks passed `meter_readings`, `agent_test_journey`, `hook_event_identity`, `project_fairness` and the Traffic storage suite.

Final-review repair: the named detector test reads the daemon's separately negotiated, job-scoped local workspace compound and verifies the TEST and BUILD members' own localities and the UNKNOWN/OBSERVE admission without a Traffic reservation; legacy four-field output remains the projection. It also uses the built CLI against a peer that rejects the capability and checks that peer received only the probe, not member details. The same target proves cache misses, hits and edit invalidation with an `ARC_DB_PATH` under `/tmp` and with `TMPDIR` covering the usual state directory. In the latter case the bounded cache is in daemon memory. The IPC unit checks cover the legacy projection and malformed compounds; 30 focused CLI metadata tests, 18 classification tests and a CLI old-peer unit test passed. Scoped Clippy passed with warnings denied for the IPC library, CLI binary and tests, daemon binary and detector test target. The runner must recheck all integrated gates on this changed tree.

The first memory-cache attempt missed on its second read; carrying the bounded entry as a serialized JSON string repaired the repeat-read path. A later detector run failed because the fixture peer inherited a nonblocking accepted socket; explicitly making that socket blocking repaired the fixture, and the target passed afterward. One intervening fixture build failed because its separately compiled execution module lacked the new module registration; the registration was added and subsequent focused checks passed.

Serena tools were unavailable in this session; source navigation used targeted local searches and scoped reads. The detector target's new assertions were moved into existing-target helper modules to keep its root file below 500 lines.

An initial attempt at the last test used `-p arc-meter` and matched zero tests; the arc-storage command above matched and passed the intended test. `git diff --check` passed for this report.

Recovery now binds a missing end footprint to `unavailable` in the same SQLite transaction that abandons the recovered job and releases its permit. A late authenticated completion cannot replace that state; a previously stored reading wins. The Meter target captures the original observer's directory, kills that observer while its end walk is held, observes the original permit released and a successor mutate the directory, then observes the late walk measure the changed allocation, lets the late completion commit and verifies the original end remains unavailable. A storage regression checks rollback of both release and binding on a forced write failure, active-job preservation and restart. The ordinary overlapping grant, completion and deadline cases remain in the named target.

The native CPU-gap fixture requires the old false-complete condition instead of accepting a partial legacy tree. One run observed a 26,066 μs unwaited burner absent from every sample, a complete legacy tree, 0.023757 s waited CPU and 0.076721873 s sampled root CPU; the negotiated individual total and unwaited CPU and the project aggregate were partial. The root does not wait for the burner, which is automatically reaped. The deterministic projection and aggregation checks remain as complementary evidence.

An earlier runner invocation of `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` exited successfully on 2026-11-23, but its 1,411.5 s wall time exceeded the 1,200 s gate. Its binary report attributed 883.3 s to 109 reported test binaries and left 528.2 s outside their reported durations; Cargo reported 1.11 s of test-profile preparation. The source-input hashes before and after that invocation matched. Its log recorded the three named production-path tests, the existing G-01 test, NFR-001 and NFR-005 passing; it measured `process_attribution` at 34.68 s, `meter_quality` at 30.74 s and `detector_workspace` at 6.67 s. This is historical evidence; the fresh post-prewarm run is reported below.

Earlier focused profiling measured first launches of newly linked `arc-storage` test binaries at about 2.5 s each and saw `cargo test -p arc-storage` take 37.4 s initially versus 15.4 s on repeat, with about 14 s reported inside tests on both runs. The operator's later measurement found 214.7 s to start 100 built test binaries once with `--list` and 0.01 s on repetition. Cold starts are therefore a supported explanation for part of the unclassified remainder, not a measured allocation of all 528.2 s. An earlier focused comparison found that optimizing third-party dependencies improved the selected `managed_exec` test body, and the existing `[profile.dev.package."*"] opt-level = 2` setting remains in place; workspace crates retain their debug settings. One earlier `process_attribution` run took 134.30 s under heavy host load, so the target's runtime remains load-sensitive.

After that receipt, the operator changed the integration runner to prewarm built test binaries and stop identified leftover journey fixtures before remeasuring the unchanged test inputs. The check logged at 2026-11-23T12:22:59Z built 100 test binaries in 0.8 s and started each once with `--list` in 221.1 s, but reused the earlier 1,411.5 s receipt. The runner was then revised to force a fresh measurement. Its 2026-11-23T12:39:11Z `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` run exited 0 in **1,072.5 s**, below the 1,200 s limit. Its binary report records 897.9 s inside test binaries and 174.6 s outside their reported durations; it does not assign that remainder to a specific cause. The three new targets passed in 34.48 s (`process_attribution`), 30.20 s (`meter_quality`) and 6.42 s (`detector_workspace`). The same log records the existing G-01 test, NFR-001, the NFR-002 host test `bounded::native_probe_then_full_100000_attempt_workload`, and NFR-005 passing.

The runner's subsequent fixed Bun command passed 372 tests across 22 files. Its traceability check passed with reconciled phase counts and no missing or duplicate IDs. Coverage passed for all 11 crates, ranging from 90.68% to 100% against the 80% threshold; the architecture harness passed with `SKIP_COVERAGE` unset after validating that coverage receipt. Those workspace, traceability, coverage and harness receipts record source input hash `7d452ffeb601a48d01a6dd305c9ebe21180e1501ba826d3aa9841a639d146236` for the earlier tree. The final-review repairs changed source afterwards, so those receipts and the 1,072.5 s timing are historical evidence, not current-tree gates. The runner must supply new formatting, Clippy, workspace timing, Bun, traceability, coverage, harness and NFR-001/NFR-002 evidence. The independent QA file remains unchanged. No customer or revenue effect is claimed.

Boundaries of the evidence. The performance values are from one macOS host
with a fixture workload and a debug build: measurements of one run, not
benchmarks or field results. The idle CPU window is 30 s, not a long-run
average. The NFR-003 bound is asserted against a no-op started through the
same shell as the hook; the absolute hook P95 and the difference against a
bare spawn are recorded in closing.md and are below 50 ms, without the
fivefold margin the asserted bound has. In the two journeys the installed
hook with a payload derived from the recorded Claude Code event stands for
the vendor, and Cargo, Bun and Vitest are offline stubs; no live vendor
session, vendor tool timeout or real toolchain was exercised. The fairness
test cancels 97 of the 100 queued builds after the fair grant is observed.
AT-MTR-01 rests on an `arc-desktop` test that this integration read and did
not run. The Phase 1 statuses are lower bounds: the sources could not be
searched here, so a case without an identified test counts as remaining. No
customer or revenue effect is claimed.

registered command, TypeScript reader), not one end-to-end test; the desktop

local Build and never accepts `LOCAL_CONTAINER`. No customer or revenue

Boundaries of the evidence. The Reaper cases run the built CLI, a real child
and `arc-reaper-daemon-fixture`, which shares production startup and adds an
injected clock, a pass trigger and named faults; no test runs the resident
`arc-daemon` with `ARC_REAPER=process` on its own interval, and the Reaper
stays off unless that switch is set. Foreground protection is reached only
through an injected fault, and the lease and protected conditions are written
into the ownership tables by the test because no production writer exists
yet. The crash tests lose an uncommitted transaction that is still in SQLite's
page cache; a transaction large enough to spill uncommitted frames into the
WAL is not exercised. Scheduled (Traffic, Governor) jobs are not registered
with the Reaper.

errors, remain unverified. Runner-owned workspace tests, desktop checks,

On that preceding repair's edits, the offline CLI and daemon fixture build passed; the combined integration tests passed 5/5, endpoint-lane tests 3/3, isolated unresolved stubs 1/1, shell capture tests 2/2, retained simulated-denominator tests 2/2 and CLI classification compatibility tests 7/7. The subsequent runner workspace check failed on an outdated Kubernetes shell-capture expectation; the arc-cli test target also failed in two isolated reruns. Its focused route test passed while checking the retained API fact, UNKNOWN/OBSERVE and isolation across two parsed leaves in either order. Both existing capture-merge tests also passed. At that snapshot, the Docker and SSH cases still read the host interface inventory through `getifaddrs`; the later repair and its focused check are described above.

The independent [QA report](../../.arc-verify/endpoint-capture/qa.md) also identifies a separate static deadline risk on its reviewed snapshot: the 1,800 ms preparation deadline starts before endpoint capture, which can wait up to five seconds. On this repair snapshot, `exec/run.rs` and `exec/preparation.rs` still have that ordering and `arc-ipc/src/client.rs` stops before connecting if the deadline has elapsed. A delayed capture could therefore cause a healthy daemon to be bypassed; no delayed-capture runtime reproduction was performed here. The operator assigned that product gap to a later cycle. QA describes its own earlier snapshot and is not refreshed by this integration repair.

The [held-permit test](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/managed_exec/capture_integration/held.rs#gh_and_buildx_same_execution_bypass_held_permit_and_buildx_cli_is_metered) runs a separate local `cargo build` fixture through `arcctl` until its child reaches a barrier. While its Build permit is held, the same direct gh execution is recorded REMOTE_CI and an offline Buildx execution is recorded REMOTE_EXEC; each stub runs once and neither adds a queue entry or reservation. The Buildx job's recorded Meter summary exposes its local CLI child's CPU usage separately; its native waited-descendants scope is Partial and does not measure remote builder CPU. The focused [protocol-1 compatibility tests](../../crates/arc-cli/tests/classification.rs#older_peer_receives_a_legacy_request_and_unproved_remote_stays_unknown) passed. No live service or vendor execution was used.

**Historical selected rows Complete: 1 of 6** (AT-TRF-04). DET-005, SAFE-008 and NFR-007 remain Partial because the complete remote-compute/official-trigger inventory has not been established. AT-TRF-02 and DET-001 retain the gaps above. The latest integration repair starts from Actor snapshot `ee20ae6ac65647488a4c0aadbc69a1092e9e10b9` (tree `8e2e15125ffb122480331c168f1c9cd9b9d42c42`); its edits are uncommitted on that snapshot. The shared-deadline test first verifies two distinct completed exit-17 lifecycle records, then a synchronized peer advances that CLI clock after the first detail request and checks that no second lookup opens. The production binary retains its system clock and 250 ms budget. Final-input runner checks remain pending.

Latest focused checks: `cargo build -p arc-cli --bin arcctl --bin arc-exec-fixture -p arc-daemon --bin arc-daemon`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` passed. `cargo test -p arc-daemon --test traffic_cli` passed 78/78 in 62.96 seconds; no new test binary was added. The two traceability Bun files passed 29 tests and `scripts/coverage/accounting.test.ts` passed 32. Intermediate Clippy runs caught type-complexity and unused-unit lints in the repair; both were fixed. These focused checks do not replace the runner's final-input gates. The latest earlier workspace receipt recorded 1,052.642 seconds on different inputs, above the 360-second goal.

Prior repair verification on uncommitted edits to Actor snapshot `a48338e6804d0ae083324faf98e9919a1d5441d4` (tree `c2e0230a9114c8c4345bdbce1f62ea5092488395`) used `oma-backend`; Serena was unavailable, so named paths were read directly. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` passed; Clippy emitted nonfatal `ts-rs` attribute notices. `cargo test -p arc-cli --bin arcctl` passed 111 tests, `cargo test -p arc-cli --test binary` passed 20, and `cargo test -p arc-daemon --test traffic_cli` passed 78 in 62.32 seconds. The two daemon cases each passed alone and while two owned CPU-load processes ran; those processes were reaped. Those prior edits exercised the source-including `arc-exec-fixture` status entry. The traceability Bun tests passed 29, and its validator reconciled all 316 rows. These are historical results; coverage, integration, desktop and full-workspace verification are runner-owned.

The shared CLI dispatcher connects the Claude and Cursor implementations and passes events through when duplicate JSON keys, fractional values or integers outside the exact integer representation prevent a safe whole-input update. The earlier agent-hooks snapshot used unconditional pass-through; the current handlers rewrite the two exact literals `cargo build` and `bun run build` on their supported local surface and pass other commands through. The unchanged VND-007, TRF-001 and AT-TRF-01 row evidence below describes the earlier snapshot. An earlier Actor reported Claude Code `2.1.326` and Cursor `3.29.9` with `--version` only. [Claude's reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks) says a replacement input is evaluated against permission rules and auto mode may deny it; [Cursor's reference](https://cursor.com/docs/hooks) documents cloud execution of project hooks and a permission-bearing response whose `ask` is not enforced for `preToolUse`. Neither version observation nor the fixture response proves live vendor acceptance.

The focused daemon test `cargo test -p arc-daemon --test traffic_bun_scripts agent_hooks_` passed two controlled event-fixture orders in 6.87 seconds on the first attempt's edited snapshot (two isolated worker entries were ignored by the outer harness and exercised by their parent tests) using real Cargo and Bun/Vite, a production daemon, synchronized FIFO release and SQLite permit transition witnesses. Each recorded zero second starts before release, high-water one, one start per command and zero active permits at completion; SQLite and CLI diagnostics were reconciled with the direct IPC read. [Registered desktop-bridge variants](../../apps/desktop/src-tauri/tests/traffic_bun_scripts.rs) use the same cases and remain runner-owned and unverified in this Actor workspace. The [CLI semantics fixture](../../crates/arc-cli/tests/binary.rs) compares direct and transformed commands for cwd, supplied non-UTF-8 environment and lineage, stdin, stdout, child stderr and exit 17. Review found its previous stderr assertion allowed arbitrary extra bytes. The revised assertion requires the exact child message once and permits only the complete known `TRACKING_UNAVAILABLE` diagnostic, or no diagnostic because delivery is best effort. The revised target passed on this repair's working tree. Its executable tool fixtures are not real Cargo or Bun binaries.

The runner's earlier Bun inventory gate identified unused `crates/arc-cli/src/hooks/claude.rs` and `crates/arc-cli/src/hooks/cursor.rs` stubs as unclassified source. The dispatcher already imports the delivered `agent_hooks` implementations, so this repair removes the obsolete stubs rather than changing the inventory classifier. The runner must repeat its integrated gate on the delivered repair.

Repair checks: `cargo test -p arc-cli --test binary` passed 16 tests in 2.38 seconds, with two isolated worker entries ignored by the outer harness and exercised by their parents. `bun test scripts/coverage/accounting.test.ts` passed 30 tests, including all three inventory and freshness cases that failed the earlier runner gate. `bun test scripts/requirements/traceability-parser.test.ts scripts/requirements/traceability.test.ts` passed 29 tests and reconciled the phase arithmetic. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` passed; Clippy emitted nonfatal `ts-rs` attribute notices. These focused results are not a substitute for the runner's integrated gate or final-input coverage.

At the earlier inspection, the mission source worktree's HEAD was the accepted baseline and the two obsolete stubs and `contract.md` were untracked there, although all three were tracked in that Actor snapshot. Their Git blob hashes matched that snapshot: Claude stub `0f4137bcad6a72bc0d83c2ff68781e6ae3066c6a`, Cursor stub `315d8dcb3b083a1b629ee6462e637196fdb76d39`, and contract `164dca7434e7295453209fd7bde89832fcee75c3`. In the current Actor snapshot, those obsolete stubs are absent and both `contract.md` and `qa.md` are tracked. The QA report remains the once-written record of its own reviewed snapshot under the operator's 19:55Z ruling. A direct read of `av.yaml` returned `Operation not permitted`, so configured container targets and their health remain unconfirmed.

The integration Actor used `oma-backend`. On the first attempt's edited snapshot, `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` passed (Clippy emitted nonfatal `ts-rs` attribute notices). Focused `arc-cli` targets passed: `--bin arcctl` 108 tests, `--test binary` 15, `--test agent_hooks_claude` 10 and `--test agent_hooks_cursor` 27. The separate ignored worker entries were exercised by their parent tests. The two traceability Bun files passed 29 tests, and `git diff --check` passed outside inaccessible `av.yaml`. These are prior-attempt results, not a runner receipt for this repair.

An intermediate focused rerun failed both orders after a cleared HOME caused the `mise` Node shim to reject its untrusted configuration. The fixture now resolves the real Node executable and explicitly supplies its test PATH; both orders subsequently passed. The actor temporarily linked the mission workspace dependencies to run the real-tool fixtures and removed those two helper links before delivery.

The performance-lane handoff reports focused repairs for Python SIGINT readiness, killed-owner witness publication, isolated SIGPIPE restoration, owned-tree stop observation and same-run Meter calibration; at that handoff the recent-fifty test still used the real clock. It records these follow-ups for QA: `go_ordered_pairs` at `crates/arc-daemon/tests/traffic_native_tools/witnesses.rs:138` (synchronization timeout), `interrupted_go_fixture_reaps_native_descendants` at `go/cleanup.rs:54` (empty PID parse), `swift_direct_timeout_terminates_and_reaps_the_owned_tree` at `swift/process_tests.rs:168` (Other instead of TimedOut), and `scripts/harness/coverage-reuse.test.ts:263` (default timeout). Further work includes the Go descendant-registration gap, unbounded fixture-lock acquisition, Meter/notice clock injection and descendants retaining probe output pipes. Under the operator's installer ruling, the post-comparison race, cross-user/privileged invocation, restrictive ownership/ACL case, watchdog descendant cleanup and vendor schema forms not written by the installer remain installer-hardening follow-ups. These are recorded for the concurrent QA owner; they are not final-input verification results.

The operator reported a prior clean workspace run in 1,047.615 seconds, with integration, desktop, post-workspace hygiene, all 11 coverage floors and same-input harness reuse passing on an earlier tree. The under-six-minute goal remains unmet and is deferred to the next cycle. Final-input runner checks and configured-container health remain unverified on this repair. Production literal rewrites were undelivered on that earlier repair snapshot; the current bounded rewrites are described above. Live vendor compatibility remains unproven. No adoption, savings or revenue were measured.

## Meaning of status, evidence and cycle

| Status | Assessment | Interpretation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Complete | Complete | The stated requirement behavior or catalog decision has affirmative focused execution evidence for the delivered supported implementation. This is not a final-gate, entire-phase, Windows or full-product completion claim. |
| Partial | Partial | Concrete execution/source evidence covers a named subset; the remaining SRS scope is stated. |
| Pending | Unverified | Implementation/measurement may exist, but the required current execution, calibration or external observation is not established. |
| Pending | Outstanding | The full obligation has no delivered acceptance in this mission and remains future scope. This does not assert that all related source is absent repository-wide. |
| Blocked | Blocked | A required prerequisite prevents assigned acceptance. No primary row is placed here merely because broader work is outside this slice. |

The Assessment column separates **unverified** from **outstanding** while preserving task-13's exact status vocabulary. Tables below report both forms. Each row's Cycle identifies the assessment that last updated its evidence. Historical cycle receipts are retained separately in the provenance ledger with their own dates, heads and verification boundaries; they never silently certify current source.

Earlier evidence cells use repository-root-relative paths; source links are relative to this document. Amendment 15's maintained validator checks only declaration/row ID completeness and uniqueness and inventory/phase/status arithmetic. Narrative, provenance, selectors, cross-reference reasons and cycle labels do not affect validity; independent review assesses their truth. Historical receipts remain historical, and current focused checks do not establish final-gate completion.

Supported scope includes direct local Cargo build/test/clippy/fmt, supported one-shot Bun tests and bounded Bun-script graphs, plus the finite native-argv Python TEST forms documented in [managed execution](../managed-exec.md#python-test-commands): `pytest`, `python -m pytest` and `python -m unittest`, discovery, core selectors and lexical virtual-environment executable paths. Python uses negotiated protocol-1 capabilities and the existing TEST window, global resource budget, SQLite writer, managed execution and Meter. Unresolved Python forms remain non-blocking UNKNOWN/OBSERVE bypass; literal command-text classification is unchanged. Pressure in deterministic Critical scenarios is explicitly injected through the production daemon/Meter seam; separate actual SystemSource observations show macOS Normal sampling. Native mixed-tool fixtures establish correctness, not responsiveness, adoption or profitability. No native Critical OS event, calibrated throughput/latency benefit, human usability study, external browser launch, distribution or revenue result is inferred. The [native contract](../../.arc-verify/native-tool-families/contract.md) adds only `go build/test/vet`, `swift build/test`, `cmake --build` and `ctest` native argv, with finite selectors/options/paths and a single `traffic.native-tools` extension. The shared [ordered descriptors](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/traffic_native_tools/descriptors.rs) enumerate 14 same-class arrival-order cases through both daemon and registered readers; current execution and environmental failures remain explicit in gates.

## Primary phase rule

Primary phases follow [SRS §22](ARC_SRS_2026-11-06.md#22-구현-로드맵-및-단계별-definition-of-done), not priority P0/P1/P2. Phase 0 owns core/IPC/locality foundations; Phase 1 owns basic Meter/Traffic and this Governor slice; Phase 2 owns full vendor/tool-family extension; Phase 3 owns Sweeper, ownership/deletion and telemetry consent; Phase 4 owns adaptation, detailed UX/accessibility/localization and performance polish; Phase 5 owns stable cross-platform release/packaging and final platform budgets.

A cross-phase obligation has one primary owner for counting. Examples: DET-003 is Phase 2 because its complete nine-family catalog exceeds the Phase 1 direct-tool subset; TRF-011/POL-005 are Phase 4 despite the Phase 1 Critical hold; NFR-018 and NFR-021 are Phase 3 because their complete scope includes Sweeper/GC; AT-GUI-02 is Phase 4 because its full Settings catalog includes learning; NFR-017 and G-08 are Phase 5 final-platform obligations. Cross-reference appearances never add another primary row or completion.

| NFR-001 | Daemon idle CPU · 일반 워크로드에서 장기 평균 **0.5% 미만 목표** | [§18, L2425](ARC_SRS_2026-11-06.md#18-성능운영비기능-요구사항-nfrslo) | None — §21 declares no dedicated acceptance-test heading for this individual obligation. | Phase 0 | Complete | Complete | [nfr_001_idle_daemon_cpu_below_half_percent_of_one_core](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/phase0_targets.rs) asserts that the idle real daemon consumed less than 0.5% of one core, from OS user plus system CPU time over a window holding at least one idle Meter tick. Measured in this cycle: 0.021% (6.29 ms of CPU in a 30.0 s window after a 3 s settle, 2 idle Meter ticks at the 15 s cadence); one macOS host, fixture workload, debug build. The window is 30 s, not a field long-run average. See [closing](closing.md#phase-1-entry-assessment). | phase-1-entry-2026-11-22 |
| NFR-002 | Daemon idle RSS · **50 MB 미만 목표** (플랫폼별 측정) | [§18, L2426](ARC_SRS_2026-11-06.md#18-성능운영비기능-요구사항-nfrslo) | None — §21 declares no dedicated acceptance-test heading for this individual obligation. | Phase 0 | Blocked | Blocked | [native_probe_then_full_100000_attempt_workload](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/bounded.rs) asserts settled idle RSS below 50 MB on an isolated real daemon. Measured in this cycle: 8.04 MB maximum over 100 samples in a 10 s window after a 5 s settle; one macOS host, fixture workload, debug build. The remaining case is external: the result on each other required platform, not run or claimed. See [closing](closing.md#phase-1-entry-assessment). | phase-1-entry-2026-11-22 |
| NFR-003 | Hook 추가 지연 · 단순 bypass 명령 P95 **50ms 이내 목표** | [§18, L2427](ARC_SRS_2026-11-06.md#18-성능운영비기능-요구사항-nfrslo) | None — §21 declares no dedicated acceptance-test heading for this individual obligation. | Phase 0 | Complete | Complete | [nfr_003_installed_hook_bypass_p95_overhead_within_50_ms](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/phase0_targets.rs) asserts P95(installed hook) minus P95(reference) at most 50 ms over 300 interleaved samples each, with a pass-through `git status` payload; the reference is a no-op started through the same `/bin/sh -c` as the hook. Measured in this cycle: hook P95 10.34 ms absolute, reference P95 7.96 ms, difference 2.39 ms; against a bare no-op spawn (P95 1.89 ms) the difference is 8.45 ms. One macOS host, fixture workload, debug build. See [closing](closing.md#phase-1-entry-assessment). | phase-1-entry-2026-11-22 |
| NFR-004 | IPC 왕복 · 로컬 작은 메시지 P95 **20ms 이내 목표** | [§18, L2428](ARC_SRS_2026-11-06.md#18-성능운영비기능-요구사항-nfrslo) | None — §21 declares no dedicated acceptance-test heading for this individual obligation. | Phase 0 | Complete | Complete | [native_probe_then_full_100000_attempt_workload](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/bounded.rs) asserts P95 at most 20 ms over 10,000 small exchanges after 200 warm-up exchanges on an isolated real daemon. Measured in this cycle: P95 0.080 ms (P50 0.066 ms, maximum 0.246 ms); one macOS host, fixture workload, debug build. See [closing](closing.md#phase-1-entry-assessment). | phase-1-entry-2026-11-22 |
| NFR-005 | Job Classifier 지연 · 캐시된 일반 명령 P95 **50ms 이내 목표** | [§18, L2429](ARC_SRS_2026-11-06.md#18-성능운영비기능-요구사항-nfrslo) | None — §21 declares no dedicated acceptance-test heading for this individual obligation. | Phase 0 | Complete | Complete | [nfr_005_cached_ordinary_classification_p95_within_50_ms](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/phase0_targets.rs) asserts nearest-rank P95 at most 50 ms over 1,000 classification round trips of `cargo build` per request form after 200 warm-up, on one connection to the real daemon, after three untimed built `arcctl classify` runs. See [closing](closing.md#phase-1-entry-assessment). | phase-1-entry-2026-11-22 |

## Acceptance-test inventory

These 47 rows refer to actual §21 Gherkin headings. GV-N and G01–G13 below are additional mission case keys, not replacements for AT IDs. In particular, **AT-GUI-03 changes a Sweeper switch**; Governor G05 does not execute it. AT-TRF-01 requires real vendor Hooks and `bun run build`; the one-slot Governor sequence does not certify that breadth. Mapped requirement IDs are reciprocal with the requirement inventory. Three UI cases map to source-only UI clauses/catalogs, with explicit reasons rather than a fabricated requirement ID.

## Other declared catalogs

The SRS also formally declares decisions, goals, ADRs, metric definitions and risks. These **104 entries** have separate primary rows and counts. Closing a technical-stack decision does not close its related product goal; a risk with a tested mitigation can remain Partial. Related IDs are descriptive mappings and are never inserted into the Requirements or Acceptance cross-reference inventories.

| R-001 | Risk register | 벤더 Hook에서 명령 변경 불가 · 강제 스케줄링 범위 축소 · Guard + Managed Launcher + Observe 등급 별도 구현 | [§25, L3382](ARC_SRS_2026-11-06.md#25-위험제약대응-책임) | VND-004; ADR-006 | Phase 2 | Pending | Outstanding | Pending: No delivered full-catalog acceptance for this entry; its decision/metric/goal/risk is not completed by analogous Governor behavior. | governor-2026-11-13 |
| R-002 | Risk register | Codex 공개 마켓 Hook 제한 · 스토어-설치 단일 경로 불가 · 공개 패키지와 사용자 승인 로컬 통합 분리 | [§25, L3383](ARC_SRS_2026-11-06.md#25-위험제약대응-책임) | VND-003; AT-PLG-02 | Phase 2 | Pending | Outstanding | Pending: No delivered full-catalog acceptance for this entry; its decision/metric/goal/risk is not completed by analogous Governor behavior. | governor-2026-11-13 |
| R-003 | Risk register | Hook timeout / fail-open · wrapper 진입 실패 가능 · 짧은 Hook 응답, 관리형 프로세스에서 큐 대기 | [§25, L3384](ARC_SRS_2026-11-06.md#25-위험제약대응-책임) | TRF-007; NFR-003, NFR-014 | Phase 1 | Blocked | Blocked | [HOST cases 1 to 3](closing.md#remaining-phase-1-inventories) have named passing tests: the hook answers without waiting, also while the window is full (`nfr_003_installed_hook_bypass_p95_overhead_within_50_ms`, `at_trf_05_nested_hook_test_shares_the_root_test_permit`); the wait happens in the managed process the hook returned (`closing_exec::stages::mixed_bun_stages_via_hook`, `local_container_buildx_via_hook_exec`); a failed hook or an unreachable daemon blocks nothing (`at_trf_06_installed_hook_cargo_test_fails_open_exactly_once`). Case 4, a queued wait observed against a live vendor's tool timeout, is external and was not run or claimed. | phase-1-entry-2026-11-22 |
| R-004 | Risk register | OS 자원 제어 차이 · hard cap 제공 범위 차이 · OS backend + Capability Matrix + admission fallback | [§25, L3385](ARC_SRS_2026-11-06.md#25-위험제약대응-책임) | NFR-017; ADR-012 | Phase 0 | Blocked | Blocked | [two_real_builds_queue_fifo_and_bypass_remote_and_unknown_while_full](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/traffic_cli/measurement.rs) is the macOS admission fallback: with no OS hard cap, admission holds the second real build at zero launches until the first releases. | R-006 | Risk register | 원격 Docker Build 오분류 · 원격 CI 잘못 제어 · Builder endpoint 판정, 불명 시 observe | [§25, L3387](ARC_SRS_2026-11-06.md#25-위험제약대응-책임) | DET-011; AT-TRF-04 | Phase 2 | Pending | Outstanding | Pending: No delivered full-catalog acceptance for this entry; its decision/metric/goal/risk is not completed by analogous Governor behavior. | governor-2026-11-13 |

### All declared toggle keys

Every key in SRS §15.5 appears individually. These 30 keys use their actual dotted identifiers, not invented hyphen IDs, so they are a supplementary inventory rather than part of the validator's 104 formal catalogs. `governor.enabled` is Partial at full-SRS scope because “all control and automatic cleanup” exceeds this first slice. Existing Meter/Traffic behavior does not imply independently implemented `meter.enabled` or `traffic.enabled` switches.

The synchronized primary sequence records child-count vector **[1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1]**. B has zero starts while queued. A's original permit is unchanged by pause; B/C/D have UNRESERVED mode and no permit generation. Scheduled A/E/F end with three RELEASED permits and **zero active permits**. Each actual target exits 17; SQLite outcome, CLI failed/exit status, registered bridge and stored Meter reports agree. Root Meter reports explicitly retain `unobserved_descendants` and do not claim complete descendant accounting. A/B/C finish before the controlled persistence restart; this sequence alone is not the complete live-scheduled-job crash/reconnect test.

The switch trace is **ON/0 → OFF/1 → restart OFF/1 → ON/2**, with SQLite enabled/revision/update timestamp matching CLI and the registered bridge. The recovery cases separately preserve historical committed receipts even when current switch revision is newer. A lost response or absent receipt never proves rollback. The actual SystemSource smoke is separate from injected Critical pressure; its CPU/RSS samples are not a representative idle benchmark.

## Focused Python TEST acceptance evidence

The [immutable Python contract](../../.arc-verify/python-tests/contract.md) bounds this slice at accepted HEAD `6b94c0bd3b309ae7b5d7ce4c3ebef50b62b5e85c`. The [documented grammar](../managed-exec.md#python-test-commands) agrees with the [entrypoint/core-selector fixtures](../../crates/arc-detector/tests/python_tests.rs#python_entrypoints_and_core_selectors_are_test), [unresolved/budget fixtures](../../crates/arc-detector/tests/python_tests.rs#unresolved_python_forms_observe_within_budget) and [no-execution/no-import fixtures](../../crates/arc-detector/tests/python_tests.rs#python_classification_never_executes_or_imports). These map DET-003/004/008 without broadening literal-text classification or executing tools during classification. Matching-interpreter probes occur only in the offline execution-fixture setup.

The native and registered targets import the same maintained scenario module; the registered target supplies the production Tauri command handler through its MockRuntime shell, without GUI/browser interaction. The four TEST pairs map DET-003/004, TRF-001/002 and the supported subset of AT-TRF-01; semantics map TRF-005 and AT-TRF-07. These are supplemental evidence references, not new requirement/AT IDs or additions to the primary inventory.

The [shared contention assertions](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/traffic_python_tests/contention.rs) synchronize actual test-body entry and explicit release for real pytest, `cargo test --offline -- --nocapture` and `bun test` in distinct offline project roots. Every second command has zero executable/body starts before release; the persisted TEST-permit interval sweep has high-water one; both commands execute exactly once and both permits release. SQLite, actual CLI and registered diagnostic/Meter reads reconcile the same IDs, classifications, outcomes and permit generations/states. A separate BUILD/Python TEST case proves the existing global CPU budget; it is not substituted for any required TEST-window pair.

The [retained Python gates](../../.arc-verify/python-tests/gates.json) record 7/7 passing tests, including matching Python/pytest readiness. The [retained Python QA](../../.arc-verify/python-tests/qa.json) separately record 7/7 Python tests and 29/29 registered-command tests; their [shared mixed-pair scenarios](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/traffic_python_tests/contention.rs) retain the job/permit facts. Shared scenarios and overlapping commands are not summed as unique product acceptance. These are delivered focused execution receipts, not final-input gate/coverage/QA verdicts.

Real console/module/unittest discovery and selector cases compare direct/wrapped success, failure, exits 0/1/17, exact stdout/stderr/stdin, cwd/environment, original executable/argv/import behavior and real venv paths. Lifecycle receipts additionally cover Governor pause/resume, fresh injected Critical pressure, queued cancellation with zero starts, native/forwarded signals, nested permit/Meter lineage, lost acknowledgments and owned daemon restart. AT-TRF-05/06/09/10 received that narrower evidence and stayed Partial in that cycle; AT-TRF-05 and AT-TRF-06 were later completed against their own lists in the [Phase 1 entry cycle](#phase-1-entry-integration-evidence). Negotiated protocol-1 diagnostics expose Python facts; legacy projections stay unresolved without changing SQLite facts. Synthetic unsupported/hung/absent peers establish separate compatibility/outage observations with one original execution; they are not described as production-daemon faults or as new deadline proofs. No responsiveness, adoption or profitability result follows from these fixtures.

## Focused native tool-family evidence

The immutable [amendment-18 contract](../../.arc-verify/native-tool-families/contract.md) freezes seven command/class combinations and both arrival orders for each against an existing same-class Cargo peer (14 descriptors). The [Go](../../crates/arc-detector/tests/native_go.rs), [SwiftPM](../../crates/arc-detector/tests/native_swift.rs) and [CMake/CTest](../../crates/arc-detector/tests/native_cmake.rs) grammar fixtures include positive/negative, checked-budget and poisoned-executable/project cases. Classification never evaluates project code. These references add evidence mappings, not primary SRS IDs.

Task-5 repairs the supported-runner workspace failures without altering the native grammar or admission interface. The [controlled-clock admission regression](../../crates/arc-cli/src/exec/governor/admission/tests.rs) checks that busy retries retain their original deadline, a healthy queued reply renews it, and the same possibly committed identity remains uncertain at expiry. The real authenticated-socket fixture now accepts complete frames on macOS and tolerates an already-disconnected peer. The [SQLite-busy contention fixture](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/traffic_cli/robustness.rs) pauses after the real queue acknowledgment, verifies the locked writer returns DB_BUSY, observes two queued CLI poll sockets, and releases the lock without a timed sleep. Signal delivery to the held lifecycle fixture is published atomically. Focused checks and failures are recorded in native gates. The exact full workspace run, including Swift-backed cases, desktop build/smoke, final-input coverage and unchanged harness passed in the supported runner on input `6d6e57e7ce3fc8259c33470c4c96135ffcc39879198974d23c35f1b2bfd9298c`; the next evidence-only check remains required.

The Swift cleanup fixture now publishes its branch and intended `cat` PIDs atomically, verifies both parent-child identities, and waits for the FIFO reader and its blocked thread before testing cleanup. This avoids mistaking the short-lived marker-publishing process for the intended descendant. For intended Swift BUILD failures, the plugin generates the compiler-error source only after its native build-tool barrier is released; held and completed cases assert the generated source's absence and contents respectively. The real SwiftPM [stream checks](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/traffic_native_tools/swift/streams.rs) require UTF-8, the controlled payload, ordered frames, their expected channel and matching frame bytes; independently varying vendor progress is not compared. The [synthetic forwarding fixture](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/traffic_native_tools/swift/forwarding.rs) separately verifies exact bytes and order on both channels through production admission, including loss, reordering and corruption of progress-shaped lines; it is not a real SwiftPM execution. The CLI's controlled-clock recent-job traversal tests all 50 supplied outcomes and expiration of the shared deadline; the restored baseline real-daemon test requires the expected persisted outcome for each of all 50 rows. The [full CLI busy regression](../../crates/arc-cli/src/exec/transport/preparation_tests/native_tools/busy/deadline.rs) uses a virtual clock and a scripted peer returning Busy through expiry, checks no early child and one eventual execution, and retains the identity and witness. The repaired real SwiftPM comparisons passed in the supported runner. The independent QA artifact records its refreshed review and F13; the operator deferred F13 to the next reliability cycle.

The supported runner's exact `cargo test --workspace` passed at 15:05–15:25 UTC on input `162a656aebba25b2bb32f81e73bf94f0ac0ce88128b82771254876fa659fcb2b`, including the corrected real SwiftPM tests. Its following Bun gate failed because the coverage inventory stopped a test-only `let` at the struct literal's closing brace. The [existing inventory parser](../../scripts/coverage/source.ts) now consumes such statements through their semicolon; [regressions](../../scripts/coverage/accounting.test.ts) preserve production lines, omit the test-only timing lines and reject shared-line ambiguity. Focused accounting passed 29 tests. This parser and test repair changed the measured input, so that workspace pass, the failed Bun receipt and the older coverage collection are historical. The subsequent supported run passed on input `6d6e57e7ce3fc8259c33470c4c96135ffcc39879198974d23c35f1b2bfd9298c`.

### Remaining test reliability risks

The mission-owned `native_recovery::owned_tree_tests::owned_tree_unwind_during_initial_capture_still_cleans_the_descendant` in `crates/arc-cli/tests/governor/native_recovery/owned_tree_tests.rs:329-347` failed with `cannot freeze fixture process` from `crates/arc-cli/tests/governor/native_recovery/owned_tree_cleanup.rs:186-215` in the coverage run `target/av-runner/coverage/runs/2026-11-15T17-36-25-687Z-c3098fad-26c9-4d1e-8d61-b0519debd232/06-command.json`. QA finding F13 documents the intermittent fixture failure; its underlying cause remains unconfirmed. The operator's 19:35 UTC ruling defers repair to the next reliability cycle and does not treat this recorded item as a blocker for this mission.

Both coverage runs failed overall; the earlier failure was the Swift cleanup fixture, not this test. The respective command records and full output are in `target/av-runner/coverage/runs/2026-11-15T15-59-20-291Z-748a4fc2-97bb-4bf7-986e-a8a17eb06770/06-command.json` and `target/av-runner/coverage/runs/2026-11-15T16-19-30-713Z-3cbe5c28-34dc-4f3a-b4dd-19a6ad3e1815/06-command.json`. Under the operator's class-2 ruling, this unchanged test remains for the next reliability cycle. The later supported run collected current-input coverage and passed the unchanged harness using its reuse receipt.

The pre-existing `db_worker::traffic::witness_repair_tests::owner_killed_after_grant_before_spawn_releases_from_initial_witness` in `crates/arc-daemon/src/db_worker/traffic_witness_repair_tests.rs:189` is unchanged from the accepted baseline. On input `296885f8d1805809b24209ba5b662f2d5a8b0e28f4083f8d2d11e8338930bdf4`, the exact workspace run starting 14:32:23 UTC failed on an absent legacy witness; the diagnostic run starting 14:33:41 UTC passed that test on the same input. Receipts: `target/av-runner/gates/cargo-test-workspace.failed-2026-11-15T14-32-23-967Z.json` and `target/av-runner/gates/cargo-test-workspace-all.json`; the latter used `--no-fail-fast`. This intermittent baseline test remains for the operator's next reliability cycle.

The restored baseline `terminal_outcomes::recent_fifty_lifecycle_exits_are_resolved_with_the_shared_deadline` in `crates/arc-daemon/tests/traffic_cli/terminal_outcomes.rs:38` passed during the exact run starting 13:40:33 UTC and failed with `outcome unknown` during the diagnostic run starting 13:58:37 UTC on identical input `a1d9fdf983f0a606ae4f1f201068acf32b7fa93e7cb42403492f93c1acfe3846`. Receipts: `target/av-runner/gates/cargo-test-workspace.failed-2026-11-15T13-40-33-593Z.json` and `target/av-runner/gates/cargo-test-workspace-all.failed-2026-11-15T13-58-37-554Z.json`. Both workspace runs failed elsewhere or overall; the later passing diagnostic used a weakened test and is not evidence that the original passed. The operator's disk cleanup overlapped the failure; its cause is unconfirmed. The restored test subsequently passed in the exact workspace run starting 18:19:30 UTC on input `6d6e57e7ce3fc8259c33470c4c96135ffcc39879198974d23c35f1b2bfd9298c`.

Both [daemon acceptance](../../crates/arc-daemon/tests/traffic_native_tools.rs) and [registered desktop acceptance](../../apps/desktop/src-tauri/tests/traffic_native_tools.rs) import the same real-tool scenarios. Offline fixtures prepare private writable caches/build trees and native barriers, and are designed to compare original command semantics and reconcile job IDs, class/reason, outcome, reservation and Meter facts through SQLite, CLI and the selected registered reader. The supported runner passed real SwiftPM frame checks and the exact workspace gate on input `6d6e57e7ce3fc8259c33470c4c96135ffcc39879198974d23c35f1b2bfd9298c`; pair IDs, job/permit facts, output hashes and failures retain their recorded scope in [gates.json](../../.arc-verify/native-tool-families/gates.json). The Actor sandbox cannot run SwiftPM. The next supported evidence-only check and final review remain outstanding.

The existing BUILD, TEST and LINT classes cover respectively Go build/Swift build/CMake build, Go test/Swift test/CTest, and Go vet. Their default windows are one each; the shared default budget is 2000 CPU millicores and 4 GiB, with the daemon-start CPU override described in [managed execution](../managed-exec.md#shared-admission). Supported native commands inherit `ARC_TRAFFIC_LINEAGE` without injection. Current nested clients use live-ancestor sidecars, and the daemon verifies ancestry before reusing a permit; the environment value by itself is not authorization. The focused [Go regression](../../crates/arc-cli/tests/governor/native_recovery/native_environment.rs) compares absent, empty and caller-supplied values against direct execution and covers scheduled, nested and protocol-1 fallback routes. It checks that a nested client prefers the live sidecar over a changed environment value and that an unrelated process presenting a copied claim waits for the held slot. A [synthetic executable route matrix](../../crates/arc-cli/tests/governor/native_recovery/native_routes.rs) checks all seven classifications and the three lineage states through production admission without running SwiftPM. Real Swift-backed execution passed in the supported runner on the input recorded above.

Task-7 environment repair, Actor snapshot `64121e9476741a4612284e75d3d6d02a02cbd30e` (tree `c4f778c7a26785a9052cd72606c19285791a447d`), adds identity-bound fixture registrations and retains the unreaped session leader until owned children are enumerated. Its PID/session reuse, early-unwind and orphan checks are fixture-safety evidence, not final-gate or stream acceptance. The Actor's focused checks from the repository root passed:

## Final verification handoff and remaining scope

Historical Python handoff (retained from its curation stage): [gates.json](../../.arc-verify/python-tests/gates.json) and [qa.json](../../.arc-verify/python-tests/qa.json) are **forthcoming destinations, not produced verdicts or current passing evidence** at task-6 delivery. Task-7, the exclusive backend gate owner, receives these two documents only for necessary final-evidence corrections. It consumes applicable current-input AV integrationCheck successes without duplicate successful runs, retains all six frozen commands and affected desktop checks, completes final inputs before collecting coverage successfully once, and reuses that run's `reuse.json` through `ARC_COVERAGE_RECEIPT` in the unchanged harness with `SKIP_COVERAGE` unset. All eleven crates must independently reach 80% production-line coverage. The immutable Python contract is included in coverage identity; generated gates/QA are excluded. Independent `oma-qa` follows completed backend gates. Only `contract.md`, `gates.json` and `qa.json` belong under `.arc-verify/python-tests/`; this curation adds no validator or report file.

The exact document check remains:

```sh
bun test scripts/requirements/traceability-parser.test.ts scripts/requirements/traceability.test.ts
```

Task-7 owns the existing `bun scripts/requirements/traceability.ts` CLI and both test files above against this actual matrix, consuming matching AV successes where applicable; there is no skip, altered argv, acceptance weakening or replacement with prose counts. Affected build/smoke and final test execution retain the mission's 7200000 ms per-check allowance. The Curator runs no native gate or executable traceability/coverage check and performs no delivery outside the worktree. The Governor task-9/task-10 verification statements in the curation record below are retained historical evidence, not current Python gate claims.

Remaining broader release work is explicit in the rows: Sweeper/GC and safety execution, all thirty switch controls beyond the delivered master scheduling subset, eleven-vendor coverage/market installs, learning, Windows and broader tool families, signed distribution, real responsiveness/usability/idle-SLO measurements and complete metric attribution. No Actor/operator implementation choice is requested. Missing evidence is a handoff boundary, not invented authorization or external effect.

## Curation record

The following Governor task-9 record is historical; the current Python task-6 scope and handoff are described above.

Skill used: `oma-docs`, with its shared execution policy and verify-command guidance. Scope is only `docs/requirements/traceability.md`. Relevant source/receipts were read directly because deferred Serena tools are unavailable. Native search was confined to documentation/non-code evidence; no implementation, tests, manifests, OMA definitions or mission state were edited.

`git status --short` and `git rev-parse HEAD` inspected preceding changes and confirmed the accepted HEAD. The initial worktree had 119 staged Governor deliverables and no matrix. `av.yaml` reported Operation not permitted during status; it remains uninspected and no workaround was attempted. The first stdout-to-JSON fingerprint attempt failed because a Git diagnostic was mixed into the captured stream; a second lookup assumed /tmp although the platform uses a different temporary directory. A corrected cached-path-only snapshot retained all 119 hashes/index entries in local temporary storage; no repository file was changed by those failed bookkeeping attempts.

`oma docs verify --help` returned exit 0 and confirmed that verification always regenerates `docs/generated/doc-refs.json`. Running it would exceed the single-file assignment, so the automated drift scan/sync was not run. This report uses **manual reference inspection — `oma docs verify` did not run**. HTTP(S) URLs are not needed as acceptance evidence and no external URL scan is claimed. Source anchors, JSON pointers, non-ignored path visibility, inventory arithmetic, reciprocal mappings and unchanged preceding file/index bytes are checked without native gates. The first `bun scripts/requirements/traceability.ts` format check exited 1 with six Complete rows linking observations without an affirmative execution selector; all inventories and phase sums already reconciled. The document was corrected by linking the relevant actual passing case/command, and by downgrading two metric catalog claims whose exact cumulative/named projections were not established. No validator or test was edited. The final handoff reports the rerun and preservation-check outcomes; task-10 owns formal test execution.
